# Assessment A — LLM Design Review: the Testing Centre

Inspected live at `http://localhost:8080/testing`, both doors, light and dark, 1280x900 and 375x812, all three dropdowns exercised. Every contrast figure below is computed from `getComputedStyle` against the composited rendered background, not from token values.

---

## (a) AI slop verdict

**Not slop at the room level. Defaulted at the panel level.**

Absolute bans, all clear: no side-stripe borders (the door border is 1px on all four sides, `testing-centre.css:45`); no `background-clip:text` anywhere in the 376-line stylesheet; no glassmorphism; no hero metric (`.testing-score` at 56px exists in the CSS but belongs to `PracticeTestRun`, not this page); no modal — the doors are an inline disclosure with `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, which is the right first thought.

**Category reflex, altitude 1 (theme and palette from "LSAT test prep" alone): passes.** The predictable answer for LSAT prep is navy-and-gold trust signalling or dark academia. What renders is a warm paper ground (`#ffffff` over `#fcfcfc`, hairlines at `#f3e3d6`) in Outfit, inherited from the classroom. Nobody guesses that from the category.

**Altitude 2 (aesthetic family from category plus "not a dashboard, not an LMS"): partly guessable.** Given those anti-references, "quiet editorial lobby, two bordered cards, 11px uppercase eyebrow labels at `0.08em`, a hairline three-column table" is close to the first thing a competent model would reach for. `.testing-shape-row` (11px tracked-caps `b`, 14px `span`, right-aligned muted `em`) is a very common quiet-editorial trope.

The tell is the seam. The **lobby is designed**: the notch tether, the door triad, the honest empty-state copy at `TestingCentre.tsx:122-127` ("No PrepTest in the bank currently has the two full Logical Reasoning sections...") reads like a person wrote it. The **interior is stock shadcn**: open the section door and you get five stacked `label + 44px rounded field` rows on a `space-y-8` rhythm, a `bg-muted` segmented pill, a divider, and a right-aligned ink button. That interior would be guessable from "build an LSAT section picker" in one shot. Nobody would say "AI made this" without hesitation, but a designer would say "somebody designed the outside and defaulted the inside."

---

## (b) Nielsen's 10 heuristics

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 3/4 | `Working` (`TestingCentre.tsx:87-98`) is a real `role="status"`, but the section door's four status strings (`SectionSelector.tsx:343, 391, 394, 399`) are plain `&lt;p&gt;` with no live region and appear and vanish in silence. |
| 2 | Match with the real world | 3/4 | "Take a section" / "Take a practice test" is excellent. "Status → Fresh · never attempted" is house vocabulary promoted to the first control a student meets. |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 2/4 | Discard deletes an in-progress sitting on one click, no confirm, no undo (`TestingCentre.tsx:290-293`). Section door has Cancel; practice-test door has none. |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2/4 | Two disabled-button treatments, two PrepTest label formats, two spellings of the same accommodation ("Time and a half" `SectionSelector.tsx:493` vs "Time &amp; a Half" `practiceTest.ts:41`), and "Standard" meaning two different clocks in the two doors. |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3/4 | Genuinely strong: stranded selections are dropped (`SectionSelector.tsx:221-233`), the RC pace mapping is total, the pre-start line states the commitment. Undercut only by Discard. |
| 6 | Recognition over recall | 2/4 | 54 and 59 undifferentiated rows. The freshness map is computed (`SectionSelector.tsx:119-139`) and never shown on a row. No marker for assigned, sat, or recommended. |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 3/4 | Type-to-filter with substring (not fuzzy) matching is the right call and well argued. The timing listbox has no arrow-key navigation and no `aria-activedescendant`. |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist design | 3/4 | Lobby is clean. The panel carries "· 77 questions" on ~50 of 59 rows and offers two accommodations one line above a note saying the student has none. |
| 9 | Recognise, diagnose, recover | 2/4 | Error copy is good ("Couldn't check which sections you've already worked. Every section is listed until it can.") but renders identically to loading copy and is never announced. |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 2/4 | "Blind review" is promised (`SectionSelector.tsx:519`) and defined nowhere. Nothing explains the break, the fourth unscored section, or whether a sitting survives a closed tab. |

**Total: 25/40.** Band: middling. Typical of a real shipped interface, and below the bar this repo's own design record sets for itself.

---

## (c) Cognitive load

| # | Check | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | One primary decision per screen | **FAIL** — the section door presents five decisions before Start |
| 2 | ≤4 visible options at a decision point | **FAIL** — 54 and 59 combobox rows (soft: a search field mitigates); five controls visible at once in the panel |
| 3 | No unexplained jargon | **FAIL** — "blind review" (undefined), "Status → Fresh" |
| 4 | A default for every choice | PASS — Status, Timing and pace all default; only PrepTest/Section are deliberately empty |
| 5 | Progressive disclosure | PASS — doors disclose panels; the RC index is lazy (`TestingCentre.tsx:58-78`) |
| 6 | Consistent element behaviour | **FAIL** — see heuristic 4 |
| 7 | No working memory across steps | PASS — every choice stays visible in its trigger |
| 8 | Irreversible actions confirmed | **FAIL** — Discard |

**Failure count: 5. Band: critical (4+).** Two of the five (2 and 3) are soft; the load is real but the number reads slightly worse than the surface feels.

Options at each decision point: doors 2 (fine); section type 2 (fine); Status 2 (fine); **PrepTest 54 or 59 (flag)**; Section 2-4 (fine); Timing 4 (fine).

---

## (d) Emotional journey

**Entry is calm and correctly weighted.** Title, one line, two doors, nothing else. "Official LSAT material, timed like test day" is honest and slightly ominous, which is right for the room.

**First anxiety spike is self-inflicted, at the moment of commitment.** `TestingCentre.tsx:299-309` scrolls the panel with `block: "start"`. Measured at 1280x900 with the section door open: the window scrolls to its own maximum (`scrollY` 313.6 of a 313.6 max), `.classroom-title` lands at viewport top **-233.6px**, and the door row is 78% off screen (`top: -124.2`, `bottom: 35.7` on a 159.9px card). The only thing left of the door you just pressed is its "ONE SECTION · 35 MINUTES" line. You press a button and the room's name and your own choice both leave the screen.

**Second spike: 54 identical rows.** A student who does not already have a policy for which PrepTest to sit has to invent one at the door of a 35-minute clock. The product knows their history and does not use it.

**Third, smaller sting:** the timing menu offers "Time and a half · 52:30" and "Double time · 70:00", then closes with "You have no extra time." Offering an accommodation you do not have, one row above the note that tells you so, is a needless small humiliation on a high-stakes surface.

**Peak is the practice-test shape table** (`TestingCentre.tsx:173-198`). Section 1 / 2 / 3 with question counts and clocks, then "77 scored questions, with a ten-minute break after the second section," then the short-RC caveat that explains a shortfall in plain terms without apologising. That object makes a two-hour commitment concrete before you take it, and it is the single most reassuring thing on the page.

**Ends are uneven.** The section door ends on a genuinely good sentence: "All 25 questions, in their published order. The clock starts straight away. It is scored when you submit, and blind review comes after." The practice-test door ends on a disabled ink-filled button that reads as enabled. Peak-end says the bigger commitment gets the better middle and the worse ending.

**Reassurance and undo are the real gap.** Nothing anywhere says a sitting can be paused, left, or resumed. The Resume list proves it survives, but only after the fact. And the one undo-shaped control on the page (Discard) deletes rather than restores.

---

## (e) What is working

1. **The notch.** `testing-centre.css:74-85` grows a 12px rotated square from the open door's bottom edge to the panel's top. It is the cheapest possible tether, it costs literally nothing when no door is open, and at 375px where the doors stack and the distance doubles it still points at the right one. I verified it renders correctly under the second door at 375px light. This is a real design idea, not a decoration.

2. **The text/fill discipline is genuinely correct, including the hard cases.** Every muted *word* on the surface resolves from `--classroom-meta-text`. Measured: lede 5.37:1 light / 8.27:1 dark; door body and meta 5.37:1 / 8.54:1; `.testing-shape-row em` 5.37:1; combobox rows 15.71:1 on the selected-row fill light and 15.59:1 dark; the timing detail 5.00:1 on the `bg-accent` selected row, which is the exact "menu row fills darker than the panel" case `testing-centre.css:115-131` was written for. The dark menu repaint (`:155-188`) is real: `.testing-menu` computes `color-scheme: dark` and `rgb(15,14,23)`, so the white-scrollbar-on-black-menu bug is genuinely gone.

3. **The shape table and the short-RC caveat.** `TestingCentre.tsx:173-198` states the commitment as an object rather than a sentence and then explains the shortfall it knows about, in plain terms, with the number the student's score will actually be out of. That is PRODUCT.md Principle 1 done properly, and it is the part of this page most worth copying elsewhere.

---

## (f) Priority issues

### P0 — Every form control in the room fails the 3:1 boundary in light; the timing control fails in both themes

**What.** The five controls inside `.testing-panel` draw their only visible edge from shadcn's `--input` / `--border`, which the light theme never overrode. Measured against what actually renders:

| Control | Border | vs panel | vs own fill |
|---|---|---|---|
| Status / PrepTest / Section triggers, light | `rgb(230,230,230)` | **1.25:1** | 1.22:1 |
| Timing trigger, light | `rgb(224,224,224)` | **1.32:1** | 1.29:1 |
| Timing trigger, **dark** | `rgb(42,40,51)` | **1.37:1** | 1.26:1 |
| (for comparison) Status/PrepTest/Section, dark | `rgb(100,97,118)` | 3.31:1 | 3.05:1 |
| (for comparison) `.testing-door`, light / dark | `rgb(156,143,133)` / `rgb(100,97,118)` | 3.14:1 / 3.21:1 | pass |

The controls' fill is `rgb(252,252,252)` on a `rgb(255,255,255)` panel, a 1.02:1 step, so the border is the only thing saying where the control is.

**Why it matters.** WCAG 1.4.11 at 3:1 on the visual boundary of an interactive control is a defect gate here (PRODUCT.md:101), and the classroom brief names this exact failure mode: `--classroom-line` "stays what it always was: hairline separation between things, not the edge of a control." The doors on this same page got it right. The dark theme got it right for the three Selects, with a comment at `testing-centre.css:173-176` spelling out the reasoning ("Control boundaries, not surface separators: 3.31:1... which is what 1.4.11 asks for"). Light was never done, and `--border` was never done in either theme.

**Fix.** Add a non-`html.dark` sibling to the block at `testing-centre.css:155` scoping `--input` and `--ring` on `.classroom-root .testing-panel`, `.classroom-root .testing-resume` and `.testing-menu` to the HSL of `--classroom-line-strong` (`#9c8f85` → `20 10% 57%`, which measures 3.14:1 on `#ffffff`). Then either set `--border` alongside `--input` in **both** blocks, or change `SectionTimingPicker.tsx:263` from `border-border` to `border-input` so it inherits the fix the Selects already have.

`src/pages/testing-centre.css:155-177`, `src/components/drill/SectionTimingPicker.tsx:263`

---

### P1 — Discard destroys an in-progress sitting on one click, with no confirm and no undo

**What.** `TestingCentre.tsx:255-259` renders a ghost "Discard" 8px from "Resume" on every unfinished sitting; `TestingCentre.tsx:290-293` calls `deletePracticeTestRun(run.id)` and drops the row immediately.

**Why it matters.** The object being deleted can be ninety minutes into a two-hour timed sitting. `testing-centre.css:275-284` moves both buttons to a full-width row of their own below 560px, which is exactly where a thumb lands. PRODUCT.md's operating context says students use this on phones in short check-ins. This is the only genuinely destructive control on the surface and it has the least protection of anything on it.

**Fix.** Inline confirm in place of the button ("Discard? Yes / No" swapped into the same row), or keep the single click and hold an undo affordance in the row for the rest of the session. Not a modal: this room has none and does not need one.

`src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx:255-259`, `:290-293`

---

### P2 — Opening a door throws the page heading and both doors off the screen

**What.** Measured at 1280x900 immediately after clicking "Take a section": `scrollY` 313.6 (the document maximum), `.classroom-title` at viewport `top: -233.6`, `.testing-doors` at `top: -124.2 / bottom: 35.7` on a 159.9px card. The page scrolls to its own bottom; 78% of the door row and 100% of the title and lede are gone.

**Why it matters.** The comment at `TestingCentre.tsx:295-298` explains the intent honestly (the panel was landing under the fold and the click read as a no-op), but `block: "start"` over-corrects into the opposite failure: the student loses the room's name and the door they chose at the moment they commit. It is also the largest single motion on the page, on the surface where a clock is about to start.

**Fix.** `block: "nearest"`, or scroll only when `panelRef.current.getBoundingClientRect().bottom &gt; window.innerHeight`, and clamp so the pressed door's `.testing-door-title` stays on screen.

`src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx:299-309`

---

### P2 — The two doors disagree about what a disabled primary looks like, and one of them looks enabled

**What.** Section door: "Start Section" disables to `bg-muted text-muted-foreground`, measured `rgb(109,106,103)` on `rgb(242,242,242)` = 4.80:1, and reads inert. Practice-test door: "Begin practice test" is a shadcn primary disabled by `opacity: 0.5` over `rgb(18,18,18)`, measured 5.21:1 label on a near-black fill. On screen it reads as the page's live primary CTA. Press it and nothing happens, and nothing tells you a PrepTest is missing.

Worse in the same pair: `PRACTICE_TEST_PACES` (`src/lib/practiceTest.ts:39-44`) is a fixed list off 35:00 that never reads `settings.defaultTimingMode`, and the practice-test door has no `AccommodationNote` and no settings link. So **"Standard" means the student's own clock in one door (`SectionSelector.tsx:250`) and a flat 35:00 in the other**, and the same accommodation is spelled "Time and a half" in one (`SectionSelector.tsx:493`) and "Time &amp; a Half" in the other (`practiceTest.ts:41`).

**Fix.** One disabled treatment for the room (give the practice-test button the muted treatment). Add the practice-test door's equivalent of the section door's pre-start line (`SectionSelector.tsx:513-521`) so the button is never the only thing explaining itself. Derive the practice-test paces from the student's stored timing mode, or rename them so "Standard" is not two clocks in one room.

`src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx:202-206`, `src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx:533-547`, `src/lib/practiceTest.ts:39-44`

---

### P3 — Four named DESIGN.md Don'ts, all cheap

- **The door lifts on hover.** `testing-centre.css:60-63` sets `transform: translateY(-1px)` with `transition: transform 300ms` (`:54-57`, verified in the computed style). DESIGN.md Buttons: "It does not lift"; Don'ts: "Don't animate layout properties... no lift on hover."
- **`transition-all` on a control.** `SectionSelector.tsx:329` puts `transition-all duration-150` on the section-type buttons. DESIGN.md Don'ts: "Don't animate the focus indicator. A `transition-all` on a control means the ring fades in." Corroborated in the browser: keyboard focus on that button computed `outline-offset: 0px` mid-transition where the global rule at `index.css:196-199` sets 2px.
- **No `prefers-reduced-motion` block.** All 376 lines of `testing-centre.css` contain none, against DESIGN.md's "Do end every world's stylesheet with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block." The JS scroll checks the media query (`TestingCentre.tsx:302-304`); the CSS transitions and the door lift do not.
- **Dark-tuned shadow under a white popover.** `.testing-menu` computes `rgba(0,0,0,0.6) 0 4px 20px` in the light theme, which is DESIGN.md's documented "five shadow tokens tuned for a dark ground" landing under a white card. "The Flat Rule: a `box-shadow` on a Workroom surface is a defect."

`src/pages/testing-centre.css:54-63`, `:376` (end of file), `src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx:329`

---

### P2 (product) — This is a menu where Principle 2 asks for a computed step, and the coach is absent from the room

Both doors resolve into "pick a PrepTest, pick a clock, press start," fronted by 54 or 59 rows reading `PrepTest 159 · 77 questions` with only the number changing. The app already computed which sections the student has never touched (`SectionSelector.tsx:119-139` builds a complete freshness map) and spends that knowledge on a two-value filter rather than on a recommendation or a row marker. Nothing on the surface reflects an assignment, a plan, or Joshua.

Held against PRODUCT.md:37-39 ("a named human being is on the other side... Nothing should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed"), the Testing Centre is currently the surface a self-study competitor could ship unchanged. The fix is small and truthful: one computed line above the doors when there is real stored data behind it, and a marker on rows that carry an assignment. Honest silence when there is nothing to say.

`src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx:311-360`, `src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx:193-217`

---

## (g) Persona red flags

**A student with LSAT accommodations.** This is the persona the page fails hardest, and it fails them in three named places. (1) `SectionTimingPicker.tsx:130-131` labels 100% "Standard" and `SectionSelector.tsx:489-493` labels 150% "Time and a half" and 200% "Double time" — but every percentage is a percentage of *their* base (`SectionSelector.tsx:99-100`). For a student on a 52.5-minute clock, "Standard" is 52:30, "Time and a half" is 78:45 and "Double time" is 105:00, none of which is a real accommodation. The control's own labels stop being true for exactly the student they were written for. (2) The practice-test door's `PRACTICE_TEST_PACES` (`practiceTest.ts:39-44`) never reads their stored mode, so "Standard · 35 min a section" is wrong for them by default, on the longest commitment on the page. (3) That door also carries no `AccommodationNote` and no settings link, so there is nothing on screen telling them their standing setting is being ignored.

**A screen reader user.** Better served than most of this codebase, and the gaps are the ones nobody went back for. `TestingCentre.tsx:87-98` builds a real `role="status"` wrapper and its comment says why. `SectionSelector` never got it: "Loading sections…" (`:343-345`), "Checking what you have worked…" (`:390-391`), "Couldn't check which sections you've already worked." (`:393-397`) and "No untouched LR sections left. Switch to Any to sit one again." (`:398-402`) are all plain `&lt;p&gt;` that appear and disappear in silence. That last one is the message that tells them why the PrepTest list is empty. Separately, `SectionTimingPicker.tsx:284-293` declares `role="listbox"` and fills it with `role="option"` buttons with no `aria-activedescendant` and no arrow-key handler: it announces as a listbox and behaves as a stack of Tab stops.

**A first-time student on a phone, deciding tonight.** At 375px the open section door produces a 1388px document. "Start Section" sits roughly 850px below the door they pressed, behind five controls at `space-y-8` / `mb-12`. The one line that tells them what they are committing to (`SectionSelector.tsx:513-521`) renders *only after both* PrepTest and Section are chosen, so on the way down they never see it. The door they pressed has already scrolled off (P2 above). And the segmented control's selected state carries a 1.05:1 fill delta (measured `rgb(21,20,31)` on `rgb(26,25,38)` dark, `rgb(252,252,252)` on `rgb(242,242,242)` light), so on a phone in a bright room "which section type am I in" is carried almost entirely by text colour.

---

## (h) Minor observations

- The practice-test combobox appends `· 77 questions` to roughly 50 of 59 rows. The detail is near-constant and discriminates nothing; the section door's identical combobox shows no detail at all. One label format for the room.
- Elevation direction inverts between themes: light is a `#ffffff` card on a `#fcfcfc` page (lifted), dark is a `#0a0913` card on a `#0f0e17` page (sunk). Measured on `.testing-panel`.
- `testing-centre.css:305-308` styles a `data-state='break'` row that the pre-flight shape table never renders. The ten-minute break exists on this surface only in prose (`TestingCentre.tsx:186-188`), while the thing that made the sitting concrete is the table.
- The disabled Section trigger's placeholder is `opacity: 0.5` on `--classroom-ink`, measured **2.61:1** light and 5.28:1 dark. Disabled controls sit outside 1.4.3, so this is not a gate failure. It is the exact opacity-multiplying-a-colour pattern the classroom brief names, and 2.61:1 for the sentence that tells you what to do next ("Pick a PrepTest first") is worth a token.
- Also on colour reuse: the disabled "Start Section" label and the *enabled* unselected "Reading Comprehension" both compute to `rgb(109,106,103)` on `rgb(242,242,242)`, 4.80:1. Same colour, same fill, opposite meanings, 300px apart.
- The Resume row says "PT 159 / Section 2 of 3" and nothing about when it was started or how much clock remains. A student returning three days later cannot tell whether resuming is meaningful. `TestingCentre.tsx:230-235`.
- `SectionSelector.tsx:553-565` still ships a commented-out "Alternative concept variants (not implemented)" block from an earlier design pass.
- `legLabel(leg as never)` at `TestingCentre.tsx:177` is an `as never` cast in a render path.
- Interactive target sizes are fine at every size: measured at 375px coarse-pointer, all controls 44px+ tall (doors 160, segmented 48, Selects 44, timing 64, Cancel and Start 44). Focus indicators are present and visible on every control I tabbed through (`solid 2px` ink from `index.css:196-199`).
- **Copy that needs Joshua, not me.** Voice is deliberately undecided, so these are flagged rather than rewritten: the field named "Status" whose value is "Fresh · never attempted"; "blind review" used without a definition; "rather than the high seventies" at the end of the short-RC caveat; whether "sitting" is the word he wants for this; and the two spellings of the same accommodation across the two doors.

---

## (i) Provocative questions

1. The page computes a complete map of which sections this student has never touched, then spends it on a two-option dropdown. What would this room look like if that map produced a **sentence** instead of a filter?
2. Both doors end in "pick a PrepTest, pick a clock, press start." Is the real fork "one section or three," or is it "**the test I was told to sit**" versus "a test I chose"?
3. The practice-test door shows you the shape of the whole sitting before you commit. The section door shows you nothing until both fields are filled. Why does the smaller commitment get less reassurance than the larger one?
4. If a student never opens the timing control they sit at 100%. If they open it, they are shown two accommodations the note underneath already knows they do not have. What is that control doing on this surface at all, for the 95% who are not accommodated?
5. The room is called the Testing Centre and its lede is "timed like test day," and yet nothing on it mentions the unscored experimental section, how the score is produced, whether a sitting survives a closed tab, or what happens during the break. Where is a student supposed to find out what they are walking into, and should that be here or on the way out?</result>

## user

Tool result:
   shadcn controls, on the classroom's dark ground.

   This app's shadcn token set is light-only on purpose: `.dark` in index.css
   is an alias that mirrors `.light` and even sets `color-scheme: light`. The
   classroom's dark theme is carried entirely by the --classroom-* tokens, so
   any control written against `bg-background` / `border-input` keeps painting
   near-white on it. Every dropdown and outline button in this room did: white
   text on a white fill, 1.01:1, an unreadable block where a control should be.

   classroom.css already solved this once for the drill hub by re-declaring the
   token set for a subtree (.drill-observatory-launcher-panel). Same move here,
   with values taken from the classroom's own dark palette rather than the
   observatory's, so the controls match the room they are standing in.

   .testing-menu is for the portalled ones — a popover or select menu renders
   outside .classroom-root and inherits nothing from the panel that opened it.
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
html.dark .classroom-root .testing-panel,
html.dark .classroom-root .testing-resume,
html.dark .testing-menu {
  --background: 245.5 21.6% 10%;

## assistant

Browser restored: theme back to `light`, viewport emulation cleared.

## P0 fixed and verified

**The defect:** every form control in the Testing Centre drew its only visible edge from a token the light theme never overrode. The controls' fill sits 1.02:1 off the panel, so the border was the sole thing marking where a control was, and it was effectively invisible. WCAG 1.4.11 wants 3:1 on an interactive control's boundary, and `PRODUCT.md:101` makes AA a defect gate, not an aspiration.

| Control | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Status / PrepTest / Section, light | 1.25:1 | **3.14:1** |
| Timing, light | 1.32:1 | **3.14:1** |
| Timing, **dark** | 1.37:1 | **3.31:1** |
| Status / PrepTest / Section, dark | 3.31:1 (already passing) | unchanged |

Two causes, two fixes:

1. [testing-centre.css:178](projects/lsatu/src/pages/testing-centre.css:178) — the token block was written `html.dark`-only. Light fell through to the global `--input: 0 0% 90%`. Added a light sibling scoping `--input` to `--classroom-line-strong` (`#9c8f85`), the token `classroom.md` reserves for "a border that is itself the affordance". Left `--classroom-line` alone so hairline separation stays hairline.
2. [SectionTimingPicker.tsx:263](projects/lsatu/src/components/drill/SectionTimingPicker.tsx:263) — used `border-border` rather than `border-input`, so it missed the fix even in dark, where the Selects already passed.

I deliberately did **not** copy the dark block's `--ring` override into light. Light's global `--ring` is `0 0% 20%`, near-black; overwriting it with a mid grey would have bought a border fix at the cost of a weaker focus indicator. Verified after the change: focus outline still `rgb(67,64,60)` at 3px.

**Verification:** typecheck exit 0, 16/16 tests pass, and a sweep of every bordered element inside `.testing-panel` / `.testing-resume` / `.testing-menu` found **exactly four** elements changed, all of them the intended controls. No collateral.

One thing I checked and chose not to touch: the menu's search row still measures 1.32:1, but that line is the `[cmdk-input-wrapper]` `border-b` at `command.tsx:42`, a divider between search and list, not a control edge. The input itself has 0px border and is identified by its icon, placeholder, and autofocus. Under this repo's own token semantics `--border` is correct there.

## Design health: 25/40

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System status | 3 | Four status strings in `SectionSelector` are plain `<p>`, no live region |
| 2 | Match real world | 3 | "Status → Fresh · never attempted" is house vocabulary on the first control |
| 3 | Control and freedom | 2 | Discard deletes an in-progress sitting on one click |
| 4 | Consistency | 2 | Two disabled treatments, two PrepTest label formats, "Standard" means two different clocks |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Strong: stranded selections dropped, RC pace mapping total |
| 6 | Recognition | 2 | 54 and 59 undifferentiated rows; freshness computed, never shown |
| 7 | Flexibility | 3 | Timing listbox has no arrow-key nav, no `aria-activedescendant` |
| 8 | Minimalist | 3 | "· 77 questions" on ~50 of 59 rows |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Error copy renders identically to loading copy, never announced |
| 10 | Help | 2 | "Blind review" promised, defined nowhere |

**Cognitive load: 5 of 8 failing (critical band)**, though two are soft (a search field mitigates the long lists).

## Not AI slop, but defaulted inside

Both assessments agree the absolute bans are clean: no side-stripe borders, no `background-clip:text`, no glassmorphism, no hero-metric template, no modal-as-first-thought. The deterministic scan returned **0 primary findings** across all four markup files, with positive controls proving it was really scanning.

The verdict A landed on is sharper than a pass: the lobby is designed (the notch tether at `testing-centre.css:74-85` is a real idea), the interior is stock shadcn. "Somebody designed the outside and defaulted the inside."

## What I did not fix, and why

You said P0s, and there was exactly one. Ranked, still open:

- **P1 — Discard destroys a sitting on one click**, no confirm, no undo, and below 560px it sits where a thumb lands. The only destructive control on the surface with the least protection.
- **P2 — opening a door scrolls the title and both doors off screen** (measured: `scrollY` at document max, title at `-233.6px`, door 78% gone).
- **P2 — the two doors disagree on disabled styling**, and "Begin practice test" reads as enabled while inert. Same pair: `PRACTICE_TEST_PACES` ignores the student's stored timing mode, so "Standard" means their clock in one door and a flat 35:00 in the other.
- **P2 (product) — accommodated students are failed in three named places.** Percentages are applied to their base, so "Standard" and "Time and a half" stop being true for exactly the student those labels were written for.
- **P3 — four named DESIGN.md Don'ts**: door lift on hover, `transition-all` on a control, no `prefers-reduced-motion` block in 376 lines, dark-tuned shadow under a white popover.

## One correction you should have

I gave both assessments a scan command copied from `~/.claude/skills/impeccable/reference/critique.md` without testing it. `npx impeccable --json <files>` prints `[]` and exits 0: it scans nothing and reads exactly like a clean pass. The real form needs the `detect` subcommand. The same reference also says not to pass CSS, which is false, and the CSS was the only file of the five with any findings. That reference will silently produce false all-clears on every project you run it against until it's corrected.

Want me to fix the skill reference, take the P1 Discard confirm, or commit what's done so far?

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

**Browser visualization** — **required** when browser automation tools are available AND the target is a viewable page. The `[Human]` overlay tab is the user-facing deliverable; the critique is incomplete without it. Skip only if the target is not a viewable page (CSS-only file, non-browser target).

The overlay is a **visual aid for the user**. It highlights issues directly in their browser. Do NOT scroll through the page to screenshot overlays. Instead, read the console output to get the results programmatically.

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable detect --json [--fast] [target]
```

- **The `detect` subcommand is required.** `npx impeccable --json <files>` (no subcommand) prints `[]` to stdout, writes nothing to stderr, and exits 0. That is indistinguishable from a clean scan, so the omission fails silently and reports every target as passing without reading a single file. If a scan returns empty, confirm you used `detect` before believing it.
- **Run a positive control whenever a scan comes back empty.** Point `detect` at a directory known to contain findings and confirm it returns them. Cheap, and it is the only thing that separates "clean" from "scanned nothing".
- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]`. **CSS is also scanned** (non-HTML files go through regex pattern matching), and in a visual audit the stylesheet is often the only file that produces findings, so do not exclude it.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings. Advisory-only results also exit 0, so read the JSON rather than trusting the exit code alone.

=== .claude/safe-push.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Safe push to origin/main when Lovable may have committed in parallel.
# Fetches, fast-forwards or merges (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.

set -e
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

# Typecheck gate: tsc does not run during `vite build` (esbuild strips types), so a
# type error (e.g. a missing import that crashes at runtime) would otherwise ship to
# main and auto-deploy. Block the push on any compile error. Set SKIP_TYPECHECK=1 to
# bypass for an emergency push.
if [ "${SKIP_TYPECHECK:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Typechecking (npm run typecheck)..."
  if ! npm run --silent typecheck; then
    echo "[safe-push] Typecheck FAILED. Fix the errors above or push with SKIP_TYPECHECK=1." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  echo "[safe-push] Typecheck passed."
fi

git fetch origin main --quiet

LOCAL=$(git rev-parse HEAD)
REMOTE=$(git rev-parse origin/main)
BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)

if [ "$LOCAL" = "$REMOTE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Already up to date with origin/main."
elif [ "$REMOTE" = "$BASE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has no new commits. Pushing."
else
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has diverged (Lovable likely committed). Merging."
  git merge origin/main --no-edit
fi

git push origin main
echo "[safe-push] Pushed $(git log -1 --format='%h %s')"

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row {
  align-items: baseline;
  display: flex;
  gap: 12px;
  padding: 12px 16px;
}

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row + .testing-shape-row {
  border-top: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
}

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row b {
  font-size: 11px;
  font-weight: 700;
  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
  min-width: 78px;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row span {
  font-size: 14px;
  font-weight: 500;
}

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row em {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);
  font-size: 12.5px;
  font-style: normal;
  margin-left: auto;
}

/* Buttons on a row. Held apart from the em so the row's meta column stays a
   meta column: this one carries controls, not a reading. */
.classroom-root .testing-shape-actions {
  display: flex;
  gap: 8px;
  margin-left: auto;
}

/* On a phone the three columns stop being three columns. Left alone, "Section 2
   of 3" broke across two lines to make room for two buttons squeezed against
   the right edge. The reading stays on one line and the controls take their own
   row underneath. */
@media (max-width: 560px) {
  .classroom-root .testing-shape-row {
    flex-wrap: wrap;
  }

  .classroom-root .testing-shape-actions {
    margin-left: 0;
    width: 100%;
  }
}

- **P2 — opening a door throws the page heading and both doors off screen.** Measured: page scrolls to its own maximum, title at `-233.6px`, door 78% gone. `block: "start"` over-corrects; `block: "nearest"` or a clamp fixes it. Smallest fix, most visible improvement.
- **P2 — the two doors disagree on disabled styling,** and "Begin practice test" reads as enabled while inert. Same pair: `PRACTICE_TEST_PACES` ignores the student's stored timing mode, so "Standard" means their clock in one door and a flat 35:00 in the other.
- **P2 (product) — accommodated students are failed in three named places.** Percentages apply to their base, so "Standard" and "Time and a half" stop being true for exactly the students those labels were written for. This one is a correctness bug wearing a copy bug's clothes.
- **P3 — four named DESIGN.md Don'ts:** door lift on hover, `transition-all` on a control, no `prefers-reduced-motion` block in the stylesheet, dark-tuned shadow under a white popover. Cheap, mechanical.

**Two things needing you, not me:**

1. **Copy decisions.** "Blind review" is promised and never defined; "Status → Fresh · never attempted" is house vocabulary on the first control a student meets; the same accommodation is spelled "Time and a half" and "Time & a Half" in the two doors. Voice is deliberately undecided right now, so I flagged these rather than rewriting them.
2. **The design-record gap.** The Testing Centre patches six shadcn tokens but is not in `DESIGN.md`'s sanctioned scoped-world list and has no brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. That's the same shape as the failure `classroom.md` documents: code outrunning the record. Either it's sanctioned and needs writing down, or it's drift.

Also flagging again, because it costs money: **another session is doing overlapping accessibility work** on this exact surface ("Separate the 44x44 touch standard from the AA floor", "finish the control borders").

Bring the panel to the student when it opens.\n  //\n  // Anchored on the doors rather than the panel. Scrolling the panel itself to\n  // the top of the viewport over-corrected into the opposite failure: the panel\n  // is taller than the viewport, so the page ran to its own scroll maximum and\n  // took the room's name and the door that was just pressed with it. Measured at\n  // 1280x900, .classroom-title landed at -234px and 78% of the door row was off\n  // screen, at the moment a student commits to a timed section. Putting the door\n  // row at the top keeps the choice that was just made on screen, and the panel\n  // begins directly beneath it.\n  const panelRef = React.useRef<HTMLDivElement | null>(null);\n  const doorsRef = React.useRef<HTMLDivElement | null>(null);\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    if (!door) return;\n    const panel = panelRef.current;\n    if (!panel) return;\n    // Leave the page alone when the panel already starts on screen. // jsdom ships no IntersectionObserver. Anything that lazily renders when it
// scrolls into view (library card previews, the carousel) throws on mount
// without this. Nothing is ever "intersecting" here, so components that
// observe visibility stay in their off-screen state unless a test drives them.
class IntersectionObserverMock implements IntersectionObserver {
  readonly root = null;
  readonly rootMargin = "";
  readonly scrollMargin = "";

  observe() {}
  unobserve() {}

}

## assistant

The scroll fix is written and tested, but I've hit a hard blocker on the verification gate.

**Done:**
- Anchored the scroll on the door row instead of the panel, and added a guard so an already-visible panel doesn't move the page at all. The original intent (panel landing under the fold, click reading as a no-op) is preserved; it just no longer over-corrects into throwing the title and both doors off screen.

## Where that leaves the critique

Three of the original findings are now shipped: the P0 contrast gate, the P1 Discard confirm, and this P2. Remaining:

- **P2 — the accommodations bug.** This is the one I'd take next, and it's the most serious thing left: it's a correctness bug, not a styling one. `PRACTICE_TEST_PACES` never reads the student's stored timing mode, and the percentage labels are applied to their base, so for a student on a 52.5-minute clock "Standard" is 52:30 and "Time and a half" is 78:45. The labels stop being true for exactly the students they were written for.
- **P2 — disabled-button treatment**, where "Begin practice test" reads as enabled while inert.
- **P3 — four DESIGN.md Don'ts**: door lift on hover, `transition-all` on a control, no `prefers-reduced-motion` block, dark-tuned shadow under a white popover. Mechanical.

The accommodations item has a fork I can't decide for you: the **behaviour** (paces ignoring stored settings) I can fix outright, but the **labels** are student-facing copy and your voice is deliberately undecided right now. /**
 * An LR section below this is a partial capture, not a section. The bank
 * publishes only questions carrying a written explanation, so a short section
 * would misreport pacing rather than measure it. Never pad one to reach it.
 */
export const LR_FULL_SECTION_MIN_QUESTIONS = 24;

export type PracticeTestPaceId = 'standard' | 'time-and-a-half' | 'double-time' | 'untimed';

export const PRACTICE_TEST_PACES: {
  id: PracticeTestPaceId;
  label: string;
  /** Seconds per section. Null when untimed. */
  sectionSeconds: number | null;
}[] = [
  { id: 'standard', label: 'Standard', sectionSeconds: PRACTICE_TEST_SECTION_SECONDS },
  { id: 'time-and-a-half', label: 'Time & a Half', sectionSeconds: Math.round(PRACTICE_TEST_SECTION_SECONDS * 1.5) },
  { id: 'double-time', label: 'Double Time', sectionSeconds: PRACTICE_TEST_SECTION_SECONDS * 2 },
  { id: 'untimed', label: 'Untimed', sectionSeconds: null },
];

export function practiceTestPace(id: PracticeTestPaceId) {
  return PRACTICE_TEST_PACES.find((p) => p.id === id) ?? PRACTICE_TEST_PACES[0];
}

export type PracticeTestLegKind = 'lr' | 'rc';

export interface PracticeTestLeg {
  kind: PracticeTestLegKind;
  /** The section number it carried on the real PrepTest. */
  section: number;
  /** Questions in this leg — what it contributes to the raw score. */
  questionCount: number;
  /** RC only: the four passages, in published order. */
  passageIds?: number[];
  /** ISO stamp set when the student begins this leg. */
  startedAt?: string;
  /** ISO stamp set when its result is read back. */
  completedAt?: string;
  correct?: number;
  answered?: number;
  timeMs?: number;
}

export type PracticeTestStatus = 'in-progress' | 'complete' | 'abandoned';

  }, [kind, rcSections]);

  /**
   * The student's own standard clock, in minutes — 35 unless they carry extra

   */
  const baseMinutes =
    settings.defaultTimingMode === '52.5' ? 52.5 : settings.defaultTimingMode === '70' ? 70 : 35;

  /**
   * Load every question the student has answered and fold it into per-section

   * the strength of rows that were never written.

  const { classId, loading: classIdLoading } = useClassId();
--- around 245-255 ---
      let mode: TimerMode;
      let customMins: number | undefined;
      if (timing.mode === 'unlimited') {
        mode = 'unlimited';
      } else if (timing.percent === 100) {
        // 100% IS the student's standing default, whatever that is, so hand
        // the timer the same mode it would have got before this control existed.
        mode = settings.defaultTimingMode;
      } else {
        // Everything else is a derived clock. customMinutes is consumed as
        // `minutes * 60 * 1000`, so a fractional value is exact to the second
        // and 94% of 35:00 runs for 32:54 rather than a rounded 33:00.
        mode = 'custom';
        customMins = (baseMinutes * timing.percent) / 100;

          allowedPercents={[100, 150, 200]}
          // RC's clock is a per-passage budget with only three paces, and

          labels={{ 100: 'Standard', 150: 'Time and a half', 200: 'Double time' }}

export function legLabel(leg: PracticeTestLeg): string {
  return leg.kind === 'lr' ? 'Logical Reasoning' : 'Reading Comprehension';
}

/** "35:00" — one leg's clock at the run's pace, or null when untimed. */
export function formatPracticeTestClock(pace: PracticeTestPaceId): string | null {
  const seconds = practiceTestPace(pace).sectionSeconds;
  if (seconds === null) return null;
  return `${Math.floor(seconds / 60)}:${String(seconds % 60).padStart(2, '0')}`;
}

=== runner: pace -> timer ===
 * drift apart.
 */
export function isSittingReturn(path: string | null | undefined): boolean {
  return !!path?.startsWith(PRACTICE_TEST_PATH_PREFIX);
}

function timerModeForPace(pace: PracticeTestPaceId): TimerMode {
  switch (pace) {
    case 'time-and-a-half':
      return '52.5';
    case 'double-time':
      return '70';
    case 'untimed':
      return 'unlimited';
    default:
      return '35';
  }
}

/**
 * Which run and which leg a runner was launched for.

=== createPracticeTestRun ===

export function createPracticeTestRun(
  blueprint: PracticeTestBlueprint,
  pace: PracticeTestPaceId,
): PracticeTestRun {
  const run: PracticeTestRun = {
    id: newRunId(),
    pt: blueprint.pt,
    pace,
    legs: blueprint.legs.map((l) => ({ ...l })),
    currentLeg: 0,
    // Two sections, then the break — the shape of the real sitting. A test that
    // somehow has fewer legs breaks after the second-to-last rather than never.
    breakAfterLeg: Math.min(2, Math.max(1, blueprint.legs.length - 1)),
    breakTaken: false,
    status: 'in-progress',
    createdAt: new Date().toISOString(),
  };
  savePracticeTestRun(run);
  return run;
}

export function savePracticeTestRun(run: PracticeTestRun): void {
  try {
    localStorage.setItem(storeKey(run.id), JSON.stringify(run));
  } catch {
    // A full or disabled store must not take the sitting down. The student
    // keeps answering; only resume-after-reload is lost.
  }

 * The RC leg's link. `reveal=end_of_set` because a section is sat under test
 * conditions — nothing is revealed until the sitting is over, and inside a
 * practice test that is doubly true.
 */
export function rcLegHref(
  run: PracticeTestRun,
  leg: PracticeTestLeg,
  legIndex: number,
): string {
  const secondsPerPassage = (() => {
    const sectionSeconds = practiceTestPace(run.pace).sectionSeconds;
    if (sectionSeconds === null) return null;
    const passages = leg.passageIds?.length || 4;
    return Math.round(sectionSeconds / passages);
  })();

  const params = new URLSearchParams({
    passages: (leg.passageIds ?? []).join(','),
    reveal: 'end_of_set',
    timing: secondsPerPassage === null ? 'untimed' : 'standard',
    start: '1',
    returnTo: practiceTestPath(run.id),
    ptRun: run.id,
    ptLeg: String(legIndex),
  });
  if (secondsPerPassage !== null && secondsPerPassage !== RC_STANDARD_SECONDS_PER_PASSAGE) {

The test caught a real bug in my own code: `PACE_PERCENT['untimed']` is `null`, and `?? PACE_PERCENT.standard` treats null as nullish, so "Untimed" silently became a 35-minute clock. Fixing:

Tool call: Edit
{"replace_all": false, "file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\lib\\practiceTest.ts", "old_string": "  const percent = PACE_PERCENT[pace] ?? PACE_PERCENT.standard;\n  if (percent === null) return null;", "new_string": "  // Not `??`: untimed's percent IS null, and a nullish fallback would quietly\n  // turn \"no clock\" into the standard one. Only an unrecognised id falls back.\n  const percent = pace in PACE_PERCENT ? PACE_PERCENT[pace] : PACE_PERCENT.standard;\n  if (percent === null) return null;"}

import "@testing-library/jest-dom";

// Supabase auth client reads localStorage on init; provide a full implementation
// so it doesn't emit unhandled rejections in the jsdom test environment.
const _localStore: Record<string, string> = {};
const localStorageMock: Storage = {
  getItem:    (k) => _localStore[k] ?? null,
  setItem:    (k, v) => { _localStore[k] = String(v); },
  removeItem: (k) => { delete _localStore[k]; },
  clear:      () => { Object.keys(_localStore).forEach((k) => delete _localStore[k]); },
  get length() { return Object.keys(_localStore).length; },
  key:        (i) => Object.keys(_localStore)[i] ?? null,
};
Object.defineProperty(window, "localStorage", { value: localStorageMock, writable: true });

## assistant

Pushed and verified. One thing surfaced while collecting the strings that you should see first.

## Shipped: `776f69b` "A practice test runs on the student's own clock"

The defect was worse than mislabelling. `timerModeForPace` mapped `standard → '35'` absolutely, so an accommodated student who chose "Standard" was **handed a 35-minute section on every leg of a two-hour sitting** — less time than they're entitled to, in the room that exists to rehearse test day. The RC leg had the same bug: its per-passage budget divided an absolute section length, so their passages got a share of 35 minutes.

Paces are now percentages of the student's own clock (100/150/200), the resolved seconds are stored on the run so a mid-sitting settings change can't move the clock, and legacy runs fall back to the old reading. Verified in the browser: on a 52.5 setting the door now reads **Standard · 53 min, Time & a Half · 79, Double Time · 105**, where it previously read 35/53/70. 66/66 tests pass, typecheck clean, and the three door tests fail against the old code.

One of my new tests caught a bug in my own change mid-write: `untimed`'s percent is `null`, and a `??` fallback had quietly turned "no clock" into the standard clock.

## Strings you need to word

**1. This one is now factually false — `TestingCentre.tsx:228`**

> "All four passages are there and **the clock is the real 35:00**, so the reading load is unchanged."

The door above it now says 52:30 for an accommodated student. This sentence contradicts it. The fix is mechanical (the computed clock is already in scope as `clock`), but the sentence is yours. I did not touch it.

**2. The same accommodation, spelled two ways in one room**

| Where | Strings |
|---|---|
| `practiceTest.ts:36-37` | `Time & a Half`, `Double Time` |
| `SectionSelector.tsx:493` | `Time and a half`, `Double time` |

Ampersand vs "and", title case vs sentence case. Pick one.

**3. "Standard"** — `practiceTest.ts:35`, `SectionSelector.tsx:493`. It now correctly means "my normal section," but for an accommodated student the row reads "Standard · 53 min a section," which could read as *the* standard rather than *their* standard.

**4. The rounding disagrees with itself.** The pace menu says "Time & a Half · **53 min** a section" (52.5 rounded up) while the clock elsewhere reads "**52:30**". Same duration, two numbers.

**5. "blind review"** — `SectionSelector.tsx:519`. Promised in the pre-start line, defined nowhere in the product.

**6. "Status" / "Fresh · never attempted"** — `SectionSelector.tsx:362, 383`. House vocabulary on the first control a student meets.

**7. "the high seventies"** — `TestingCentre.tsx:229-230`. Vague where the rest of that paragraph is precise.

**8. "sitting"** as the unit — `Unfinished sittings` (`TestingCentre.tsx`), plus `Discard this sitting?` which **I wrote** in the Discard confirm. If that isn't your word, both should change together.

Remaining from the critique: the P2 disabled-button treatment ("Begin practice test" reads as enabled while inert) and the four P3 DESIGN.md Don'ts.

## assistant

**I made a mistake you need to know about, and it's the one thing you'd specifically told me to avoid.**

I pushed another session's commit along with mine. `b1a3f37` ("RRC: the Report Card says what it means, and can be read by keyboard", 5 files in the Activity Centre) was committed by a parallel session **16 seconds after** mine, into this shared working tree. My push used `HEAD:main` rather than the explicit SHA I'd used on the previous three pushes, so local `main` had already moved past my commit by the time it ran, and both went up.

Root cause is mine and specific: I used `git push origin HEAD:main` instead of `git push origin ece5480:main`. The explicit SHA is exactly what prevented this on `e1b2507`, `aa6bb7e` and `dc25049`. I dropped it here.

I have **not** tried to undo it. Removing their commit from `origin/main` needs a force-push, which is destructive, and their work looks finished (it ships tests). But it went public without that session choosing to publish it, so if they weren't ready, that's their call to make, not mine. Worth a word to whichever session owns the RRC Report Card work.

## What you asked for, both shipped in `ece5480`

**The 35:00 line.** It now reads the chosen pace instead of a literal. An accommodated student saw 52:30 on the control and "the real 35:00" in the paragraph beneath it. When the sitting is untimed the clause drops out entirely rather than asserting a clock that isn't running, so it reads: *"All four passages are there, so the reading load is unchanged."*

**The two spellings.** Unified on the section door's form — `Time and a half`, `Double time`. I picked that over the ampersand/title-case pair because `AccommodationNote`, which is your prose, already says "You have time and a half" and "You have double time." The label list was the outlier, not the note. Verified in the browser: the menu now reads Standard · 35 min, Time and a half · 53 min, Double time · 70 min, Untimed · no clock.

Four tests; the two covering the caveat fail against the previous component. 59/59 pass, typecheck clean.

Two honest limits on the verification:

- **The short-RC caveat could not be exercised in the browser.** No PrepTest currently on offer appears to have a short RC section — the two lowest question counts of the 59, PT 102 at 75 and PT 152 at 76, both render no caveat. It's covered by tests instead, not by a live check.
- **A third copy of the old spelling exists** at `rcSections.ts:37-38`. I left it: `RC_SECTION_PACES` feeds only a type and a lookup with no callers, so nothing renders it. It'll reintroduce the inconsistency the moment someone wires it up.

## assistant

Saved to memory as a standing rule, indexed in `MEMORY.md`.

I checked today's shipped work against it, and it holds:

- **`sectionSeconds` on `PracticeTestRun`** was added as an *optional* field read through `practiceTestRunSectionSeconds`, which falls back for runs written before it existed. No stored sitting became unreadable. There's a test pinning exactly that fallback.
- **In-flight sittings are frozen.** The resolved clock is stored at creation and read back, so changing the timing setting mid-sitting can't move the clock under work already underway.
- **Discard got harder, not easier** — a confirm where there was a single click, and the deletion is still the student's choice, never a side effect.
- **No migration, backfill, cleanup or reset** was run against any student table.

One consequence you should know rather than discover: an **already in-progress** sitting created before this change carries no stored clock, so it keeps the old absolute reading. For an accommodated student that means a sitting they started earlier will still finish on 35-minute sections. That's the rule working as intended — I'd rather leave an old sitting on the clock it began with than silently re-time it halfway through — but it means the fix reaches them on their *next* sitting, not the current one. If you'd rather I repaired in-flight runs instead, that's a deliberate rewrite of stored student records and I'd want you to say so explicitly.

The one that did get sloppy today was the push, not the data. Both are now covered by memory: push by explicit SHA, and never let student work be the casualty of a change.

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

# Session Handoff — Testing Centre critique, then shipping its P0/P1/P2 findings

## Where it started
Session picked up from a handoff whose single OPEN item was "which surface was Joshua checking when he said he couldn't see the Testing Centre work?" I checked production before re-asking and found the work was already live; Joshua then said that question was a misunderstanding and to drop it. He redirected to: make the job properly done, run an `$impeccable critique`, verify against the visual page. That produced a scored critique, and the rest of the session was fixing its findings one at a time on his instruction.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **`e1b2507` Testing Centre controls get a boundary you can see** — WCAG 1.4.11 P0. The token block at `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\testing-centre.css` was `html.dark`-only, so light fell through to global `--input` and all controls measured 1.25:1. Added a light sibling scoping `--input` to `--classroom-line-strong` (3.14:1). `--ring` deliberately NOT set in light: global is `0 0% 20%`, overwriting would weaken focus. Also `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\drill\SectionTimingPicker.tsx:263` `border-border` → `border-input` (was failing 1.32:1 light / 1.37:1 dark).
- **`aa6bb7e` Discard asks before it destroys a sitting** — P1. Inline two-step confirm in `ResumeList`, not a dialog (this room has no modal). Focus moves to **Keep**, never Discard. Confirm resets if the sitting leaves from under it.
- **`dc25049` Opening a door stops throwing the room off the screen** — P2. Scroll anchored on `.testing-doors` not the panel, and only fires when the panel is out of reach (`PANEL_VISIBLE_MARGIN = 120`).
